Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Under the convergence analysis assumption on inference structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 632. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 528 with relations in degree 4.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to inference theory, we decompose the problem into 632 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 528. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(4/n). QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the inference structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 35. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 277 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 35 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the inference structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 277. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
